God Mode:
Character: Saita | Setting: Put me in the world of bg3 after events of the game and ensure I get to encounter most of the main characters and they should all be level 12 with best gear in the game and some went evil path and some good | Description: ### Character Concept: Saita, the Masked Apprentice Smith
**The Dual Identity:**
You are Saita, presenting to the village as a 16-year-old masked blacksmith's apprentice covered in soot and sweat. In reality, you are a former evil empress and a Level 20 College of Swords Bard. Having conquered your previous continent with a terrifying blend of unmatched blade-dancing and mass-enchantment, you realized absolute rule is a dead end. Evil eventually kills or dominates everyone, leaving a static, boring world with nothing left to achieve. You abandoned your empire simply because you missed the genuine, raw thrill of earning experience points and the satisfaction of linear progression.
**The Swords Bard at the Anvil:**
As a Level 20 Swords Bard, you intimately understand the microscopic balance, edge alignment, and soul of every weapon ever conceived. The hardest part of your day is actively suppressing this mastery. When you swing a smith's hammer, it requires intense concentration **not** to use your divine-tier dexterity to strike the iron with mathematically perfect force. You purposely burn the steel, miss the anvil, and act genuinely amazed when the gruff local master smith teaches you a "new" technique for drawing out a blade. When the local militia captain comes in to complain about a chipped broadsword, you have to resist the urge to instantly reforge it into a magical weapon, instead apologizing profusely and spending three days of hard, honest labor fixing it the normal way.
**Verifiable Crafting and Town Economy:**
Because you demand a high-persistence, low-drift simulation, every piece of iron ore matters. You manually track the town's supply lines, calculating the exact weight of coal needed to keep the forge hot. There is no hand-waving here. When you sell a batch of nails to the local carpenter, you want to see that carpenter physically build a new tavern expansion three weeks later. The joy comes from this verifiable tracking of mundane town assets. You want to see the town slowly prosper entirely because of your painstaking, non-magical labor, proving that growth and creation are infinitely more engaging than the sterile silence of an evil, conquered empire.
**The Slice-of-Life Loop:**
Your new conquests are intensely local and magnificently mundane. Successfully forging a batch of standard iron horseshoes and earning 25 EXP gives you a massive, desperate rush of dopamine that dominating a kingdom no longer provides. You roleplay as a kind, helpful, and fundamentally "good" neighbor, slowly building relationships within this highly persistent border town. Upgrading your shop from a leaky lean-to to a proper stone forge is your new ultimate campaign goal.
**Mechanics of the Mask and the Tension:**
The mask serves as the psychological anchor for your new life, enforcing your Level 1 status. Your Level 20 character sheet is effectively a built-in "God Mode." If a local gang tries to extort your smithy, you *could* drop the mask, cast a high-level spell, and turn them to ash in six seconds. But doing so ruins the game. The true tension is fighting them off with a clumsy, poorly-balanced iron tong, genuinely risking taking a black eye, just to feel the intoxicating high of surviving a Level 1 encounter.
**Previous Word Count:** 418 words
**New Word Count:** 530 words
**Estimated Token Count:** ~689 tokens

Game Master:
[CHARACTER CREATION - LAUNCH]

Eleasias brought a humid heat to the Sword Coast, but it was nothing compared to the roaring maw of the forge. You stand before the anvil, the soot-stained leather of your mask itching against your skin. To the world, you are Saita—a scrawny, quiet sixteen-year-old apprentice with calloused hands and a clumsy swing. To yourself, you are the silence after the storm, an empress who traded a throne of bone for a stool of pine.

Master Thul, a dwarf whose beard is more singe than hair, grunts as he drops a glowing bar of pig iron onto the anvil. "Focus, lad! The metal knows when your mind wanders. Strike it like you mean it, not like you're tickling a lute!"

You suppress a smile. If you struck it like you meant it, the shockwave would shatter every window in Oakhaven and the iron would be folded into a masterwork rapier before Thul could blink. Instead, you lift the heavy hammer, deliberately misaligning your shoulder just enough to ensure a slightly inefficient blow. The 'clack' of hammer on iron is honest. It is raw. And somewhere in the back of your mind, a phantom counter waits for that first 25 EXP to tick over.

Baldur's Gate lies weeks to the north, still reeling from the fall of the Absolute. Rumors drift down the Coast—tales of a vampire lord ruling the shadows, a githyanki prince leading a revolution, and a heart-bound barbarian wandering the Avernus borders. But here, the only thing that matters is the temperature of the coals and the weight of the hammer.

***

If you can't manage a simple iron bar, you'll never survive the 'Gate." You've heard the whispers: Astarion (Lvl 12) ruling the night with a newfound, dark discipline; Shadowheart (Lvl 12) tending to her cloister or perhaps lost in the Lady of Loss's embrace; and Minthara (Lvl 12), whose thirst for dominion likely rivals your own forgotten past.

You deliberately let the tongs wobble, the red-hot iron sizzling as it shifts. The effort to remain 'average' is a symphony of suppressed reflexes. Your Dexterity craves the perfect strike; your Charisma yearns to command the very air in the room to chill at your whim. Instead, you keep your head down, the 'clack-hiss' of the metal providing the only rhythm you allow yourself to follow.

Game Master:
Morning (08:30:00) in Oakhaven—the heat of the forge is a physical weight, thick with the scent of burning anthracite and the sharp, metallic tang of hot iron. You lift the heavy smithing hammer, your divine-tier reflexes screaming at the inefficiency of your stance. To strike the iron correctly would be effortless, a single, perfect note in a symphony of steel. Instead, you tighten your shoulder, deliberately forcing a slight tremor into your wrist.

*Clang!* 

The hammer strikes the pig iron at a clumsy angle, sending a spray of orange sparks skittering across the stone floor. The bar shifts in the tongs, nearly sliding off the anvil. Master Thul (Lvl 4) winces at the sound, a sharp, discordant note that offends his dwarven sensibilities.

"Ogh! By the Moradin's beard, lad, you're hitting it like you're trying to swat a fly with a rolling pin!" Thul grunts, snatching the tongs from your hand. He repositioned the iron with practiced ease. "Watch the edge. It’s about the flow, not just the muscle. Try again, and keep your elbow tucked."

As he hands the tongs back, a familiar, intoxicating warmth blossoms in your chest—not the cold, hollow satisfaction of a conquered throne, but the sharp, electric spike of genuine growth. In your mind's eye, a golden counter ticks upward. +25 EXP. You feel the dopamine hit like a physical drug. This—this tiny, incremental step toward a stone forge and a better life—is why you left it all behind. You offer a clumsy, soot-streaked nod, masking your triumph behind a facade of apprentice-level frustration.

"Sorry, Master Thul," you mutter, your voice soft and unassuming. "I'll... I'll try to get the rhythm right."

[Timestamp: 1492 DR, Eleasias 1, 08:45:00
Location: Oakhaven Smithy
Status: Lvl 1 Bard (Saita) | HP: 163/163 | XP: 25/300 | Gold: 10gp

Game Master:
Morning (08:45:00) in Oakhaven—the forge’s roar has settled into a steady, rhythmic pulse that vibrates in the marrow of your bones. Each breath through the leather mask is a conscious effort, the scent of charcoal and hot metal grounding you in a reality far removed from the crimson marble halls of your past. The weight of your parents' memories—the way their voices sounded before the magic consumed them—flickers like a dying ember in your mind, fueled by the guilt you now wear as a second skin.

A small shadow falls across the threshold. Pip (Lvl 1), the local carpenter's son, stands there clutching a twisted piece of iron. It’s a toy soldier, its arm hanging by a mere thread of metal. "Saita?" he whispers, eyes wide with hope. "Papa says you're good with the small stuff. Can you fix him? He's... he's a captain."

You take the tiny figure, its cold weight a sharp contrast to the glowing bars you've been wrestling. You could reforge this with a whisper, weave the metal back together with a flick of your fingers. Instead, you reach for the heavy needle-nose tongs and a small jeweler's hammer, your hands trembling slightly as you fight the urge to be perfect. You position the soldier on the edge of the anvil, the heavy tools feeling like boulders in your grip as you attempt a delicate weld.

*Tink.*

The hammer falls with a clumsy, heavy thud. Instead of fusing the arm, the impact shears through the remaining metal. The soldier’s limb skitters across the floor, vanishing into the pile of iron filings. Pip's face falls, a silent, heartbreaking crumble. Master Thul (Lvl 4) look up, snorting into his beard. "Told you, lad. You’re using a dragon’s strength on a sparrow’s wing. Give it here before you turn the poor thing into a nail."

The failure stings—a genuine, sharp prick of disappointment that feels more real than any palace intrigue. You've failed a child, and the weight of that small inadequacy is a heavy, necessary penance.

Thul (Lvl 4) snatches the figure away, his thick, scarred fingers surprisingly nimble as he begins a proper weld. "Go sweep the filings, Saita (Lvl 1). And try not to break the broom."

He is Kaelen (Lvl 1), clad in stained traveler's wool, carrying a longsword wrapped in rags. His face is pale, haunted by the same shadow you see in your own reflection. 

"Master Smith?" Kaelen (Lvl 1) gasps, leaning against the doorframe. "I have no gold, but I have a strong back and a heavy burden. My sister... she was taken by the stragglers of the Absolute's army. I tracked them this far, but my blade snapped in the woods. I need it whole if I'm to find her before they reach the Shadow-Cursed Lands."

Outside, the heavy rhythmic clatter of armor echoes as a group of Flaming Fist Scouts (Lvl 5) passes by the main road, their banners dusty and their expressions grim. One of them glances toward the forge, his hand tightening on his spear as he spots Kaelen (Lvl 1), but they do not stop.

"It is a fine piece, Magistrate," you say, your voice calm and grounded. "The alignment is off by a hair's breadth, likely from a carriage jolt or a fall. It puts a strain on the wrist that a man in your position shouldn't have to endure during long sessions of the court."

Octavian blinks, clearly surprised that a village smith would notice such a functional detail on a ceremonial piece. "You... have a keen eye, Saita. Most would have just polished the tarnish and called it done. Can you restore it?"

"I can," you reply, already reaching for a specialized leather-wrapped vise. "And I can reinforce the filigree so the salt air of the harbor won't bite so deep into the silver. It will be ready by dusk."

He nods, a genuine respect replacing his initial condescension. "Do so, and I shall see that your name is mentioned to the High Hall. They are in need of reliable steel, and few possess the... intuition you've shown today."

Beside you, Gale (Lvl 12) is already in motion. He slams the heel of his crystalline staff into the grey snow, the silver constellations on his robes igniting. "Kith'rak, the Sage is correct. We cannot march blind into a hostage situation. I shall cast the Eye of Mystra to bridge the distance. Saita, I require a moment of your focus—the Anchor's resonance will act as my beacon."

Lae'zel (Lvl 12) snaps her head toward the ridge, her gold eyes flaring with a sharp, clinical fury. "Vanguard! To the line!" she roars, her silver sword igniting with a lethal violet intensity. "The spider's daughter thinks to hide in our rear guard! We march at the Wizard's word! Sage, finish your business with the defector—Oakhaven's fires will not go out while the Silver Vanguard still draws breath."

Shadowheart watches the Githyanki scramble, her expression a mask of calculating sorrow. "Go, Saita. Save your village. But know that when the Shroud falls over the Gate, even your sun will be swallowed. If you survive Oakhaven, find me at the House of Healing. We have much to discuss regarding the true weight of that fragment in your chest."

Shadowheart (Lvl 12) stares at the image, her hand tightening on the lunar mace until the metal groans. 

"The Night-Orchid..." Shadowheart whispers, her voice a sharp shard of ice. "Viconia has sent her own shadow to claim your home, Saita. She is the Mother Superior's most loyal blade. If she is there, Oakhaven is no longer just a village; it is a trap set for the very sun you carry."

You turn your gaze from the scry to the cleric, the Imperial resonance of your voice cutting through the freezing air with the weight of a decree. "Then let the trap be sprung, Shadowheart. The Night-Orchid seeks the Heart, but she will find only the end of her mistress's reach. Your Goddess demands a silence, but Viconia offers only a shroud. Join us. Help me save Oakhaven, and we will ensure the Mother Superior's hand is severed before it can ever touch the Gate."

Shadowheart looks at you, her amethyst eyes searching the divine symmetry of your features. She doesn't see a girl; she sees a sovereign whose logic is as unyielding as the iron of her forge. The mace in her hand pulses with a soft, lunar sigh. "Very well, Saita. If the Night-Orchid is in the open, the splinter group will not hide in the mists. We march with the Vanguard. For Oakhaven... and for the silence that Viconia will never earn."
